115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

4
三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Positive implications for
downstream tech hardware
companies from US
hyperscalers' 3Q26 results
We view US hyperscalers’ commentary and capex guidance as
positive for the Greater China supply chain. The next watch point
is the impact of hyperscalers' CY27 capex guidance on Greater
China tech hardware companies' C4Q26 earnings.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Note: META, AMZN and GOOG are covered by

Brian Nowak. MSFT is covered by Adam Wood.
We view US hyperscaler commentary as positive for the Greater China supply
chain: The tone was uniformly positive, with demand still outstripping supply. With
CY26 nearly over, the focus was on CY27 capex. Although quantitative guidance was
not provided this quarter, US hyperscalers’ comments suggested that demand
would remain robust into CY27.
We stay OW on the Greater China supply chain: We like Wistron, a key L6 partner

to Nvidia and AMD, with new customers bringing more L10 (Lenovo and an AI lab)
and L11 AI server exposure (Amazon, Oracle). We also like Lenovo, a key general-
purpose server supplier to Microsoft and Oracle, but also a rising supplier of AI
servers, with ~US$54bn AI server pipeline as of C2Q26/F1Q27. We continue to like
Wiwynn, a key general-purpose server supplier to three hyperscalers (Microsoft,
Meta and Amazon) and a potential assembly partner for two new customers (a

hyperscaler and an AI lab). Wiwynn is also a key supplier of Trainium server racks, as
well as AMD Helios racks for Meta.  We like GCE and EMC/TUC as beneficiaries of
US hyperscalers’ demand for PCB and CCL in general-purpose and AI servers. We
like Unimicron and NYPCB, Greater China’s key ABF substrate suppliers for
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
MSFT lowered its CY26 capex guidance by US$5bn to US$170bn, reflecting
delays in third-party capacity deliveries rather than a demand issue.
MSFT also guided for capex to “accelerate” in C1H27, driven mainly by CPUs
(higher memory & storage costs), GPUs and delayed third-party capacity
deliveries.
META narrowed its CY26 capex guidance to US$135-145bn (from US$130-145bn)

and said it would keep investing “aggressively” into CY27.
GOOG maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$195-205bn and reiterated that
CY27 capex will increase “significantly” y/y.
AMZN maintained its CY26 capex guidance of US$220bn and said CY27 capex
would be “meaningfully” higher than CY26.
November 2, 2026 02:07 AM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

Wiwynn (6669.TW)
09 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Wiwynn
Valuation
Our target price for Wiwynn is NT$3,200. We value Wiwynn based on PE as we believe the company’s earnings performance
and industry dynamics will be the key focus areas for the market. Our TP is based on 16x 2027E EPS, which is at the top end of
the historical trading range since listing and above the average PE multiple of the peer group. We believe our target multiple is
justified by the company’s 100% exposure to data centers, rising scale, and robust earnings growth, as well as stabilizing ROE.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Wiwynn’s shares to trade below our target price include: 1) Potential volatility in capex
deployment; 2) Component price hike; 3) Peer competition; and 4) FX volatility.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Wiwynn Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q beat on higher GM/OpM
Recent concerns revolve around potential data center delays,
though the supply chain has not seen any changes. However, we
lower our mid-term growth  rate to bake in some conservatism.
Our PT now implies a lower CY27 P/E at  12x. RR looks attractive
at 7.7x CY27 P/E vs. the 3Y  avg. of ~10x.
4Q revenue should continue to grow double-digits q/q: Wiwynn announced
October revenue at ~NT$165B (-4% m/m, +81% y/y). If we assume the October

monthly run-rate can be sustained, 4Q should grow at least 13-14% q/q. However,
we think ASIC server racks will continue to ramp-up through-out year-end, so we
forecast 4Q revenue to ~20% q/q to reach ~NT$520B (+78% y/y). Higher cost
structure and higher mix of ASIC server racks should drive margins to decline q/q to
~8.5% (-40bps q/q), resulting in net profit of ~NT$27.7B or EPS of NT$46.70.
ASIC/GPU projects remain on track: Our checks indicate that  Trainium3 server rack

production is ramping up well at Wiwynn, and with T3, Wiwynn is a more
comprehensive partner, also engaging in UBB and switch trays. It will also engage in
AMD's (covered by Joe Moore) MI455 Helios rack assembly for customers such as
Meta (covered by Brian Nowak) and Oracle (covered by Sanjit Singh). We forecast
Wiwynn to build ~8K Helios racks for CY27.
3Q results beat on better margins: Revenue was NT$434.6B (+63% y/y) with GM
of 8.9% (-40bps q/q), which is 90bps/30bps above MSe/Street. OP reached ~NT

$32.5B (+66% y/y) for OpM of 7.5% (+20bps q/q), which is 100bps/50bps above
MSe/Street, driven by operating leverage. Non-op losses were slightly higher than
MSe/Street mainly due to higher interest expense, resulting in net profit of ~NT
$23.4B (+52% y/y) for EPS of NT$42.09, 44%/10% above MSe/Street.
Reiterate OW with price target unchanged at NT$2,515 (12x/11x CY27/28e P/E) –
our higher EPS estimates are offset by a lower mid-term growth rate. Risk-reward is

highly attractive at 7.7x CY27e or 6.7x CY28e P/E vs. the three-year range of 6-16x,
with a strong pipeline of AI and non-AI server projects.

Price target discussion and valuation methodology
We keep our price target unchanged at NT$2,515: This implies 12x CY27e P/E or 11x CY28
P/E, vs. its thee-year average forward P/E of 10.3x, which we think is fair, in view of
Wiwynn's AI server project wins and share gains for general-purpose servers in the next 1-
2 years.
We continue to use a residual income model to derive our price target. Key assumptions
include a cost of equity of 7.0% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 6% and risk-free rate of

1%), a medium-term growth rate of 4% (lowered from 6%), and a terminal growth rate of
3%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$3,210 and NT$1,470,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

電子–半導體 
環球晶(6488.TT) 
短期獲利受火災影響，但供需吃緊及漲價趨勢不變

營收走勢圖

研究員/鄭高輯 OWENCHENG@uni-psg.com

結論: 
環球晶短期獲利表現低於預期，主要原因包括: (1) 義大利 Novara
廠 7 月火災影響、(2)短期產能受限，以及(3)2H26 仍適用舊LTA 價
格 。 不過最壞狀況已過 ， 隨著時序進入4Q26， 預期營收將優於3Q26，
且自 1Q27 起 ， 隨著新產能開出 ， 以及開始適用價格調漲後的新LTA，
獲利表現將明顯轉佳 。 預估2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為17.82 元
(原估 19.49 元)、33.14 元(原估 32.53 元)。在投資建議上，儘管
短期營運不如預期 ， 但環球晶股價已大幅修正 ， 且2027 年半導體及
記憶體新 Fab 產能開出的趨勢明確(13 座->23 座)， 因此矽晶圓供需
轉緊且價格上漲的趨勢不變， 故維持買進建議 ， 然考量近期AI 供應
鏈出貨不順的雜音較多 ，漲價概念股評價受壓抑 ， 故將PBR 由 6X 調
整至 5X (PBR 歷史區間 2~6X，漲價循環合理評價應在中上緣 4~6X
之間)，將目標價由 1350 元下調至 1170 元。

重點摘要:

1.3Q26 財報表現不佳，主要受義大利 Novara 廠火災影響:  
3Q26 營收-2.3%QoQ(+2.6%YoY)，若排除 7 月義大利 Novara 廠火災
之影響，營收原本可以成長雙位數%QoQ。在毛利率方面，儘管3Q26
折舊費用略較前季下降 ， 但因Novara 火災導致部分高毛利率的 8 吋
SOI 基板出貨中斷 ， 導致毛利率較前季下滑0.6pt 至 20%， 不過在費
用控管下，OPM 較前季回升 0.4pt 至 9.8%。單季稅後 EPS 3.13 元，
低於原預期的 3.64 元 ， 且明顯低於前季的7.9 元 ， 差異主要係2Q26
認列較多 WAF 金融評價利益。

2.美光支付 5 億美元並非是預付款，而是認購 CB:  
今年 7 月環球晶與美光宣布簽訂 10 年期 LTA，美光將支付環球晶 5
億美元，然而，並非以預付款形式支付，而是環球晶發行上限 5 億
美元的可轉債，由美光擔任唯一應募人。研究員認為，客戶支付預
付款綁定產能的強制力相對較強 (預付款在出貨後會轉營收且不退
還)，然而，CB 存在股本稀釋的問題(推估約 3%上下)，相對較不受
歡迎。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3.2027 年起開始適用新 LTA 價格，ASP 將改善:  
在價格趨勢方面，公司對於價格調整將採取逐案談判 (Case-by-
case)，依據產品規格、供需狀況、合約到期時間與個別客戶情況進
行協議，非全產品線統一調漲特定百分比。公司認為目前市場環境
日益支持價格回歸合理水準，且已有多筆新 LTA 接近洽談完成，預
計自 2027 年起整體 ASP 將受惠於新 LTA 與產品組合優化而有所改
善。

3.4Q26 營收會優於 3Q26，但受限產能，且價格仍沿用舊合約:  
展望 4Q26，義大利 Novara 廠已正式啟動部分復工，且整體客戶需
求動能依然非常強勁 ， 甚至有客戶願意提前適用新價格以爭取產能 ，
但受限於產能不足 ， 且4Q26 絕大多數價格仍適用舊合約 ， 因此預期
4Q26 營收會優於 3Q26， 但仍無法達成雙位數%成長 。 不過自1Q27 起，
隨著新產能開出以及 ASP 提升，營運動能將轉佳。研究員預估2026
年營收-1.0%YoY，稅後EPS 17.82 元(原估 19.49 元，下修主要是因
為火災)。

4.2027 年營運將明顯好轉，大致維持獲利預估:  
展望 2027 年 ， 根據研調統計 ， 開始營運投片的新Fab 數量將自 2026
年的 13 座大幅增加至 2027 年的 23 座 ， 且成熟製程庫存健康化 ，電
源管理、車用與工業恢復，公司預期 2027 年矽晶圓供需環境將比
2026 年更吃緊。就環球晶而言，自 1Q27 起隨著新產能開出，且開
始適用調漲後的新 LTA 價格，將有助於營收及獲利轉強。此外，隨
著 AI 資料中心發展 ，SiC 市場正步入新成長週期 ，12 吋 SOI 晶圓也
受惠矽光子強勁動能推升需求，密蘇里州廠是目前美國唯一具備12
吋 SOI 製造能力的基地，產能持續爬升，客戶意向及預付款已可涵
蓋擬新增產能的 90%以上。基於以上，環球晶將步入產業上升循環，
預估 2027 年營收+33.8%YoY，稅後 EPS 33.14 元(原估 32.53 元)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340원에서 1,315원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026년 예상 FCF의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

작성자(한동희)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

시장과 산업의 시차. 2026 
SMR 첫걸음. 2H27 원전 수
주 확대 본격화 
허민호

minho.hur@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 85,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
56,700 
유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

KOSPI 5937.66

5  
표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

Document 294: Asia week ahead_ Philippine GDP and key data on China, Taiwan, and Japan

### Page 1

Philippine GDP is the highlight of the week, alongside China’s
inﬂation and trade data, Taiwan’s trade ﬁgures, and Japan’s
household spending and price indicators

Deepali Bhargava and Lynn Song
Asia Research highlights of the week
Democratic victories to constrain President Trump and increase tech
scrutiny
Philippine inﬂation spikes, but BSP likely to hike only 25bp in
December
AI in 2027: 20 questions for the global economy
Philippines: GDP growth to rebound on
public spending and manufacturing strength
We expect GDP growth to recover to 3.5% year-on-year in the third
quarter. This will be driven by a gradual normalisation in
government spending and continued strength in the
manufacturing sector, which is also reﬂected in robust electronics

### Page 2

export growth. However, private consumption is likely to remain
subdued as consumer sentiment stays weak amid still-soft public
spending and elevated inﬂation, particularly from higher energy
prices. While external demand and manufacturing should provide
support to growth, the recovery in domestic demand is likely to be
more gradual.
China: Trade to remain strong as inﬂation
edges lower
China will release October inﬂation and trade data. We expect CPI

inﬂation to edge down to 0.8% YoY, from 0.9% in September,
suggesting that consumer price pressures remain subdued amid
continued weakness in domestic demand. By contrast, PPI inﬂation
is expected to rise further to 5.3% YoY, from 5.0%, driven mainly by
elevated energy and commodity prices.
Export and import growth are expected to accelerate to 40.7% YoY
and 37.3% YoY, respectively amid resilient external demand for
technology-related and manufactured goods and increased

commodity imports. The trade surplus is expected to narrow
slightly to $131.2bn, from $133.5bn in September, as import
growth accelerates.
Taiwan: Technology demand supports strong
trade
Taiwan releases October trade data on Monday. We expect export
growth to moderate to 51.3% YoY, from 60.9% in September. Even
so, growth should remain exceptionally strong, supported by
demand for semiconductors and other AI-related products. Imports
are expected to accelerate to 69.2% YoY, from 51.7%, reﬂecting

strong demand for semiconductor equipment, components and
advanced materials. Despite faster import growth, the trade surplus
is forecast to widen to $25.6bn, from $23.6bn in September,
highlighting the continued strength of Taiwan’s technology export
cycle.

### Page 3

Japan: Household spending, current account
and producer prices in focus
Japan will release September household spending and current
account data. The market expects household spending to remain
weak, falling 3.1% YoY, unchanged from August, highlighting
continued pressure on domestic consumption. The current account
surplus is forecast to narrow to JPY 3262bn, from JPY 4062bn.
Japan’s October PPI will be released on Thursday. Market consensus
expects producer price inﬂation to moderate to 6.5% YoY, from

6.8% in September. Prices are forecast to rise 0.2% month-on-
month following a 0.3% decline. This suggests that upstream price
pressures are gradually easing, though they remain elevated,
keeping expectations for further BoJ policy normalisation ﬁrmly in
focus.

TUẦN 26-30/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản 
ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có m ức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 9-13
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 2.7% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 4.9%, both
posting their fourth straight week of losses. Thursday’s 4.4% plunge in the
S&P 500, its worst day since April 2025, came as investors weighed what
the Democratic sweep of Congress means for the AI data center buildout.
Both indexes rebounded Friday on strong jobs data, with the S&P 500 up
0.3% and the Nasdaq up 1.4%.
October’s CPI (Tue) and PPI (Fri) headline this week’s data calendar. Ten

Fed officials are scheduled to speak, and the UK and Eurozone report Q3
GDP. Wednesday’s Veterans Day holiday closes the bond market. Here’s
more:
(1) Earnings season. So far, 88% of S&P 500 companies have reported
their Q3 results. So 60 companies still have to report, and 13 will do so
this week. The "Earnings Squiggles" for 2026, 2027, and 2028 are likely to
rise even further over the remainder of this year (chart). Currently, industry
analysts' consensus is that S&P 500 companies’ aggregate EPS will be

$366.60 this year, $426.30 next year, and $495.10 in 2028. In the past,
Earnings Squiggles tended to fall because analysts were too optimistic.
This time, they haven't been optimistic enough!
(2) Fedspeak. Ten Fed officials are scheduled to speak this week
(Goolsbee, Barkin, Waller, Logan, Schmid, Paulson, Hammack, Daly,
Williams, and Jefferson). These are their first remarks after the
Democratic sweep. These Fed heads are likely hawkish on balance,

especially after Friday's strong employment report.

### Page 4

In early October, the federal funds rate (FFR) futures implied 3.3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months and 1.7 over the next six. Since then, those
projections have fallen to 1.9 and 1.4 (chart). Friday’s employment report
lifted the odds of a December rate hike to 78%. We think the FOMC still
needs another 50bps of increases to reach a neutral FFR.
San Francisco Fed President Mary Daly told CNBC on Friday that “the job
market remains stable and solid, but not booming,” with real wages falling

as pay lags inflation. She expects the Fed “will have to tighten policy
further.”
(3) Inflation. October’s CPI and PPI reports will be released on Tuesday
and Friday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation Nowcasting model
projects that the headline CPI rose 0.24% m/m and the core CPI 0.21%
m/m. That would hold headline CPI inflation at 3.7% y/y and ease core to
2.5% from 2.6% (chart). October would mark the 68th consecutive month
of CPI inflation above 2.0% y/y. The model sees both accelerating in

November, to 3.9% and 2.8% y/y.
The Nowcast also suggests that October’s PCED inflation will remain
firmly above the Fed’s 2.0% target, with the headline and core PCED
inflation rates projected at 3.8% y/y and 3.2% y/y, respectively. Both are
expected to heat up a bit in November.

### Page 5

October's PPI inflation is likely to be hotter than September's headline and
core readings of 6.3% and 5.0% (chart).
One of the more troublesome components of the PPI recently has been
transportation & warehousing services, which jumped 16.0% y/y in
September (chart). Soaring diesel prices are fueling inflation.

### Page 6

(4) ADP & unemployment claims. Payrolls rose 165,000 in October, well
above the 65,000 consensus. The unemployment rate fell to 4.1% from
4.2%. ADP’s weekly employment reading (Tue) follows a four-week
average of 16,750 for the week ended October 10, down from 17,500
(chart). ADP’s monthly report showed private payrolls up 86,000 in
October, beating the 67,000 consensus.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week's 189,000, with the four-week
average falling to 194,750, the lowest since 1969 (chart). Continuing

claims were 1,718,000 for the week ended October 23, with a four-week
average of 1,709,000.

### Page 7

(5) Global. The UK’s Q3 GDP (Thu) rose at an estimated 0.7% q/q pace,
up from 0.5% in Q2, when real GDP was up 1.4% y/y (chart).
The Eurozone’s updated flash estimate of Q3 GDP (Fri) follows the initial
0.4% q/q reading. Real GDP rose 1.3% y/y in Q3, with Spain leading the
major economies at 2.7% and France down 0.1% (chart).

Document 369: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP . The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP , is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026
Johanna MELKA
johanna.melka@bnpparibas.com

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 4 日

1/6 
RBA は２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 
～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念や追加利上げ期待の後退が豪ドルの重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日に開催した定例会合において、政策金
利を 25bp 引き上げて 4.85％とすることを決定した。利上げは２会合連続、年明け以降５回
目となり、政策金利は18 年ぶりの高水準となった。 
○ 背景には、原油高に加え、国内の需給ひっ迫を反映してインフレ圧力が根強く、物価上昇が
幅広い財・サービスに波及していることがある。ＲＢＡは物価見通しを大幅に上方修正し、
インフレ率が目標レンジに収束する時期も従来の想定から後ずれすると見込んでいる。 
○ 一方で、今回の利上げ決定においては、理事９人のうち３人が据え置きを支持するなど、景
気減速への警戒感も強まっている。ブロック総裁は、これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響
を見極める必要性を強調しており、ＲＢＡは当面、政策金利を現行水準に据え置き、物価や
雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。ただし、インフレ圧力が高止
まりすれば追加利上げに動く可能性も残ると考えられる。 
○ 豪ドル相場については、利上げが事前に織り込まれていたことに加え、追加利上げ期待の後
退を受けて弱含んだ。先行きについても、国内景気の減速懸念や米国の金融政策、 中国景気、
中東情勢などの影響を受けつつ、当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/6 
【RBAは２会合連続の利上げ決定、年明け以降５回目、政策金利は18 年ぶりの高水準に】 
オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日の日程で開催した定例の金融政策委員会に
おいて、政策金利であるオフィシャルキ
ャッシュレート（ＯＣＲ）を 25bp 引き上
げて 4.85％とすることを決定した （図
１）。ＲＢＡは、 ２月の定例会合で２年３
ヶ月ぶりの利上げに動き、５月の定例会
合まで３会合連続の利上げを実施した。
その後、６月会合、８月会合では金利を
据え置く様子見姿勢に転じたものの、９
月会合で３会合ぶりの利上げに動いた。
このため、今回は２会合連続、年明け以
降５回目となる。今回の利上げにより、
ＯＣＲは18 年ぶりの水準となった。 
事前の市場予想では、ＲＢＡが利上げに動くとの見方が大宗を占めた。背景には、ＲＢＡ高官か
ら先行きの政策運営について、タカ派姿勢を維持する発言が相次いだことがある。 なかでもハウザ
ー副総裁は、原油高などを理由にインフレ見通しが「著しく悪化している」として、金融政策では
供給ショックに対処できないとしつつ、 コスト上昇がインフレ期待の上昇に波及することを防ぐ必
要があるとの考えを示した。そのうえ、物価動向がＲＢＡの想定を上回る可能性が高いとの見方を
示すなど、 インフレに対する警戒感を強調した。 同氏の発言は政策運営の方向性を明示したもので
はないものの、インフレ見通しの悪化に加え、インフレ期待を抑え込む必要性を強調したことは、
金融市場に追加利上げを意識させるに十分であったと考えられる（注１）。

注１ 10 月21 日付レポート「弱含む動きをみせてきた豪ドル相場は持ち直しに向かうか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666763.html） 
図 1 政策金利(OCR)の推移

（出所）CEIC, RBA より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 3

3/6 
７-９月のインフレ率は前年同期比＋3.9％と前期 （同＋3.9％） から横ばいで推移し、 ＲＢＡが定
める目標レンジ（２～３％）を引き続き上回るとともに、ＲＢＡが８月会合で示した見通し（前年
比＋3.6％）をも上回った（図２） 。前期
比も＋1.3％と前期（同＋0.6％）から上
昇ペースが加速し、 食料品やエネルギー
など生活必需品の物価上昇に加え、サー
ビス物価も上昇基調を強める 動きが確
認された。コアインフレ率（トリム平均
値）も前年同期比＋3.7％と前期（同＋
3.6％）からわずかに伸びが加速してお
り、 ＲＢＡの見通し （同＋3.3％） も上回
っ た。前期 比も＋ 1.0％ と前期（ 同＋
0.8％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、
基調的なインフレ圧力が強まっていることがうかがえた。さらに、ＲＢＡのブロック総裁が議会証
言において、 これまでの利上げにより物価が目標に回帰させることが可能か、今回会合で議論する
考えを示すとともに、物価上昇圧力が依然強いとの認識を示した。このため、金融市場では利上げ
が実施されるとの見方が一段と強まった（注２）。

【評決は６対３に割れる、インフレ見通しを上方修正する一方、景気の下振れリスクにも警戒】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、 理事９人のうち６人が利上げに賛成した一方、 ３人は据え置き
を支持するなど、 金融引き締めの必要性を巡って判断が分かれたことが明らかにされた。 前回の９
月会合では、全会一致で利上げを決定したことを踏まえれば、今回の票割れは、インフレ抑制を優
先する姿勢が維持される一方、 景気減速への警戒が理事会内で強まりつつあることを示唆している。 
物価動向について「依然高水準にある」 、 「足元のデータはインフレの広がりを示唆している」と
の認識を示した。 加えて、 「インフレ見通しも大幅に上方修正された」 として、 その理由に 「イラン
情勢の悪化による燃料やその他商品市況の上昇を一部反映した」ことを挙げた。 一方で、 「同国内に
おける供給能力のひっ迫も想定以上にやや厳しい状況にある」 との見方を示し、「ＡＩ （人工知能）
関連投資が技術関連のインフレ圧力を招いているほか、 多くの財・サービスでインフレ圧力が依然
高い」 、 「多くの企業がコスト上昇圧力に直面するなか、財・サービス価格をすでに引き上げ、ない
し検討を行っており、インフレ率は 2027 年を通じて高止まりすると予想している」とした。その
結果、 「インフレ率が目標レンジに達するのは2027 年後半と従来の想定から約３四半期遅れる」と
して、 「インフレ期待への上振れリスクをもたらしている」との見方を示した。 
会合にあわせて公表した最新の『四半期金融政策報告』では、物価見通しが大幅に上方修正され

注２ 10 月28 日付レポート「豪ドル相場に持ち直しの兆し、RBA による追加利上げ観測が再燃」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666938.html）

インフレ率 コアインフレ率

### Page 4

4/6 
た。2026 年末のインフレ率は前年同期比＋4.5％と、 ８月会合時点の見通し （同＋3.6％） から大幅
に上方修正された。コアインフレ率についても、2027 年半ばの見通しを前年同期比＋3.5％と、８
月会合時点 （同＋3.0％） から上方修正した。 そのうえで、 ＲＢＡはインフレ率が目標レンジの上限
である３％以下となる時期を 2027 年末近くまで後ずれすると見込んでおり、目標レンジの中央値
である 2.5％への収束も2028 年半ばになるとの見方を示している。 加えて、 原油高などに伴う企業
のコスト増加が販売価格や賃金設定に波及し、 家計や企業のインフレ期待が高止まりするリスクに
も警戒感を示した。 
一方、金融環境については「年明け以降の利上げにより引き締まった」との見方を示した。その
うえで、 「経済は減速し、不動産価格も大幅に下落し、失業率も上昇している 」として、先行きは
「向こう１年で生産の伸びは一段と鈍化が見込まれ、 生産能力への圧力が緩和され、 インフレ率を
目標レンジに戻す一助になることが期待される」 とした。 また、 「金利上昇や不動産価格・取引高の
下落は、 不動産投資や家計消費を下押しする」 とする一方、 「データセンター関連投資を含む、 企業
部門による設備投資は当面堅調な推移が見込まれ」 、 「雇用も増加が見込まれるが、 失業率は８月会
合時点の想定より大幅に上昇する」との見通しを示した。 
ただし、 実体経済や物価見通しについては 「依然として不確実性が高い」 として、 「イラン情勢の
行方は見通せず、インフレ率が想定を上回り、実体経済が想定を下回るシナリオも考えられる」と
し、「不確実性が長期化すれば、 世界経済や同国の経済成長率が低下する可能性がある」 とした。 一
方で、 「足元ではＡＩ関連投資による押し上げ効果がイラン情勢の悪影響を上回っており、主要貿
易相手国の景気は想定を上回る力強さが続いている 」ものの、 「同国では生産性の伸びの鈍化が潜
在成長率の重しとなり、 不動産市場の低迷の度合いや実体経済への影響にも不確実性が残る」 との
見方を示した。 
今回の決定については 「インフレ期待が高止まりするリスクが高まっている」 ほか、 「インフレ見
通しや上振れリスクを踏まえ、 目標レンジに戻すためにはさらなる引き締めが妥当と判断した」と
した。そのうえで、先行きも「インフレ率を持続的に目標レンジに戻すために必要な措置を引き続
き講じる」として、 「経済指標や見通し、リスクに関する評価を注視する」としつつ、 「政策対応は
情勢変化に対応できる態勢が整っており、 物価安定と完全雇用の実現というマンデートに注力する」
との考えを示した。ただし、９月会合では追加利上げに言及する文言が含まれていたものの、今回
はそうした文言は削除されるなど明確な方向感を示されなかった。

【ブロック総裁は過去の利上げ効果を見極める姿勢を強調、追加利上げには慎重な構えをみせる】 
一方で、 今回の会合後に実施されたブロック総裁の記者会見では、 先行きの金融政策運営につい
て慎重な姿勢がうかがわれた。同氏は、今回の利上げ決定について、原油価格の上昇による直接的
なインフレへの対応ではなく、 従来からの国内における需給ひっ迫や、 エネルギー価格の上昇が幅
広い物価に波及する二次的な影響を抑制することへの対応と説明した。 ただし、 年明け以降に実施
した５回の利上げのうち、 直近の利上げの効果は実体経済に十分に浸透していないとして、 今後公
表される経済指標を見極める必要性を強調した。 今回の会合で据え置きを支持した理事についても、

### Page 5

5/6 
不動産市場の調整や失業率の上昇、 賃金上昇率が加速していないことなどを重視し、 金融引き締め
の効果を確認するために時間を置くべきだと判断したことを明らかにした。 
事実、 足元の国内景気にはこれまでの金融引き締めの影響が広がりをみせている。 ９月の失業率
は 4.7％と上昇傾向にあるとともに、 不動産価格の下落や住宅ローン需要が減少していることなど、
金利上昇に敏感な分野で調整圧力が強まる動きが確認されている。 ＲＢＡは今回公表した最新の経
済見通しにおいて、2027 年末の実質GDP 成長率を前年同期比＋1.3％と、 ８月会合時点の見通し （同
＋1.6％）から下方修正している。さらに、失業率も2028 年末に 5.1％まで上昇すると予想してお
り、 ８月会合時点の見通し （4.8％） から上方修正している。 同氏は、 中心的な見通しでは景気後退
を想定していないとしつつ、経済成長率の大幅な鈍化や家計消費の弱さが続くとの認識を示した。 
注目されるのは、 前述した通りＲＢＡが声明文から、 必要に応じて追加利上げを行うとの明示的
な表現を削除したことである。 同氏は、 これを金融政策の方向転換を示すものと受け止めるべきで
はないと説明しており、追加利上げの可能性を排除してはいない。ただし、理事会内において判断
が分かれたことに加え、 これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響を考慮すれば、 ＲＢＡが今後も断続
的に利上げを実施する可能性は低下したと考えられる。 当面は現行水準で政策金利を据え置き、 物
価や雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。一方で、原油価格の高止まりや
インフレ期待の上昇が続けば、2027 年前半に追加利上げに動く可能性も残る。 反対に、 不動産市場
の調整が深刻化するとともに、 個人消費や雇用の悪化が想定以上に進む場合には、 金融引き締めの
長期化を見直す必要性が強まるとみられる。

【豪ドル相場は利上げ決定後に弱含む動き、先行きも当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性】 
金融市場では、前述の通り今回の利上げが事前に相当程度織り込まれていたこともあり、決定直
後の豪ドル相場は対米ドルで下落した
（図３）。 声明において、追加利上げを明
示する文言が削除されたことに加え、理
事会内 部において 据え置きを支持する
意見がみられたことも、追加的な金融引
き締めの 実施に対する市場の見方を後
退させたと考えられる。 今後の豪ドル相
場を巡っては、ＲＢＡの政策金利が高水
準で維持されることが一定の下支え要
因になると見込まれるものの、 国内景気
の減速に伴って追加利上げ期待が後退
すれば、豪ドル高圧力は限定的となる可能性がある。 
さらに、豪ドル相場は国内の金融政策だけでなく、米国の金融政策や中国景気、国際商品市況、
世界的な投資家のリスク選好の影響を受けやすい。 仮にＲＢＡが追加利上げに踏み切る一方、 米国
の金融引き締め姿勢が弱まれば、 米国とオーストラリアの間の金利差の変化が豪ドル相場を押し上

### Page 6

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
6/6
げる可能性がある。しかし、米国でもインフレ圧力が根強く、金融引き締めの長期化観測が強まる
場合には、米ドル高が豪ドルの上値を抑えるとみられる。また、中国景気の減速や中東情勢の悪化
によるリスク回避姿勢の強まりも、豪ドルの下押し要因となり得る。こうした点を踏まえると、豪
ドル相場は短期的に一進一退の展開となるとともに、 明確な上昇基調への転換には、 豪州の景気減
速への懸念後退や米国との金融政策の方向性の違いが一段と明確になることが必要であろう。

Carsten Brzeski, James Knightley, James Smith and Rebecca Byrne
2027 could be make or break for the global economy.
Will AI unleash a productivity boom, transforming growth and the
jobs market? Or will the remarkable surge in US tech ﬁnally come
crashing down, taking business investment with it? Can Europe’s
surprising economic resilience continue, or will dramatic elections,
political brinkmanship and rising bond yields herald a return to
stagnation?
Join ING’s economists on Tuesday 10 November as we launch our
2027 Economic Outlook. We’ll take you through our biggest calls
for the year ahead, and the unexpected twists that could knock the
global economy oﬀ course. Sign up here.

Document 464: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.
